Document 030: 20261027_投資週報_AI資金壓力擾動市場，科技基本面仍穩健

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261027_投資週報_AI資金壓力擾動市場，科技基本面仍穩健.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。 隨著市場風險偏好下降 ， 避險資金轉向美國公債 ，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準 ， 法股單日下跌約1.5%， 歐元同步走弱 。 市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度 ，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇 ， 但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性 ，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變 。 隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升 ， 具技術優勢 、 財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利 ， 而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待 。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

Document 054: 2026年10月美国非农就业数据点评：10月非农：K型在收敛

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月美国非农就业数据点评：10月非农：K型在收敛.pdf
Report source: 东吴证券
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告·宏观报告·宏观点评

东吴证券研究所

2026年11月07日

相关研究  
《 “蓝色浪潮”下特朗普的政治掣肘—
—2027年度展望之海外政策》 
2026-11-06 
《黄金ETF，2026年10月复盘与11
月展望》 
2026-11-05

[Table_Tag]    [Table_Summary] 
◼ 核心观点：10月非农就业数据“供需双旺”，失业率回落。需求端，非
农就业增长趋势确认触底反弹、就业扩散指数改善、强周期性数据趋势
向好；供给端，劳动参与率进一步修复。数据公布后，市场交易美国增
长预期的改善，近期因AI交易大幅调整而来的增长预期的下修有所缓
和。总体来看，近期美国AI产业的“供需双弱”与10月非农的“供需
双旺”形成鲜明对比，美国经济呈现出K型收敛的迹象，这在一定程度
加剧了市场对美国增长预期和相关交易的难度。与此同时，中东局势仍
不明朗，油价带来的通胀风险溢价仍难以收敛。进入2027年，民主党
占多数的国会两院与共和党总统的格局下，两党围绕财政的拉扯料将成
为市场新的扰动，特朗普与民主党在财政议题上的反复料将加剧市场波
动，短期大类资产或仍缺乏明确主线。 
◼ 10月非农：新增非农大超预期，失业率回落。美国10月新增非农16.5
万，较80名分析师预期的7万高出4.8x标准差，前值9月数据从2.9
万上修至6万，前两个月累计上修8.4万，3个月平均新增非农从前值
7.9万（从5.1万上修）上行至13.7万。与此同时，在劳动参与率回暖
的同时，失业率从4.175%下降至4.141%，预期4.2%；时薪增速反弹：
平均时薪环比+0.4%，预期+0.3%，前值+0.1%，时薪同比+3.1%，预期
+2.9%，前值+3.0%。总体来看，10月非农就业数据全面向好，超预期
的非农、上修的前值和好于预期的失业率公布后，市场交易增长预期的
改善，美债利率&美元指数走高，黄金走低。由于非农代表的美国K型
经济下方的超预期，一定程度上扭转了近期AI带来的K型上方的走弱
预期。 
◼ 就业结构：多个指标显示需求趋势延续改善。10月非农就业的行业结构
在总体上仍然保持就业集中性高的特征，但一些周期性行业和就业扩散
度出现了趋势性改善：①就业增长延续集中度的较高特征：10月新增的
16.5万就业中，专业商业服务、医保分别新增7万、5.5万人，剩余行
业合计新增4万；②强周期性行业就业确认趋势改善：在本月就业高增
长的专业商业服务行业中，管理&技术咨询服务新增1.7万就业尚难以
确认持续性，但周期性较强的临时救助服务行业的就业增长基本确认了
触底反弹，其同比增速自2025年12月以来持续上行，10月录得+3.03%，
为2022年10月以来新高；③扩散指数持续改善：10月就业的1年期、
1个月期就业扩散指数分别录得52、57.6，分别连续5个月、10月在荣
枯线以上，且呈震荡上行趋势；④新增就业趋势确认触底反弹迹象：由
于非农单月数据波动较大，市场也常用3MMA（三个月均值）来衡量就
业趋势，10月非农3MMA录得13.7万，前值从5.1万上修至7.9万，
自7月以来再度呈现上行趋势；若以同比口径来看，10月ADP、私营
非农同比增速分别为0.62%、0.7%，均呈现出进一步反弹向上势头。 
◼ 供需结构：供需双旺。与9月失业率因劳动参与率回升而反弹不同，10
月就业表现出供需双旺，劳动参与率回升的同时，失业率走低。具体来
看，10月居民端就业同样保持强劲。从供需双方来看，10月新增的42.1
万劳动力中，12.8万来自人口自然增长，29.3万来自非劳动力减少；10
月新增在职者46.3万，其中42.1万来自劳动力增长，4.1万来自失业者
减少，因此，劳动参与率从上月的61.79%提升至61.91%，失业率从上
月的4.175%下降至4.141%。从失业者结构来看，周期惯性最强的永久
失业者（失业半年以上）的占比和绝对人数近期都呈现出下行趋势。总
体来看，10月非农呈现出供需双旺的局面，供需缺口从8万增至45.5
万，也对应失业率的走低；供需缺口的改善同步对应时薪的改善，但10
月时薪增速仍然保持稳定，尚不存在工资-通胀螺旋风险。 
◼ 此外，劳务供给方面一个值得注意的现象是，近期劳务供给呈现出回暖
态势，从数据来看更可能来自移民的回归，表现为新增劳动力更多是外
国劳动力而非本国、且集中于16-24岁群体。而随着中期选举的结束，

### Page 2

2 / 7 
德州的翻蓝（州长和参议院均被民主党拿下）意味着移民问题可能迎来
反转，2027年德州涌入的拉丁裔移民可能再度增多。 
◼ 展望与策略启示：美国经济呈现K型收敛迹象。综合来看，10月非农
就业数据全面向好，基本确认美国就业增长趋势的回暖，表现为3个月
均非农就业增长趋势确认触底反弹、就业扩散指数改善、强周期性的临
时救助行业和永久性失业数据均延续改善。总体来看，近期美国AI产
业的供（数据中心建设进展担忧）需（Anthropic业绩担忧等）双弱与
10月非农的供需双旺形成鲜明对比，美国经济呈现出K型收敛的迹象，
这在一定程度加剧了市场对美国增长预期和相关交易的难度，表现为美
股在AI交易降温的主导下大幅调整的同时，偏强的就业数据推高加息
预期，给美债、黄金等资产带来扰动。与此同时，中东局势仍不明朗，
油价带来的通胀风险溢价仍难以收敛。进入2027年，民主党占多数的
国会两院与共和党总统的格局下，两党围绕财政的拉扯料将成为市场新
的扰动，特朗普与民主党在财政议题上的反复料将加剧市场波动，短期
大类资产或仍缺乏明确主线。 
◼ 风险提示：油价失控推高通胀；美联储维持高利率水平时间过长，引发
金融系统流动性危机。

### Page 7

免责及评级说明部分

东吴证券投资评级标准 
投资评级基于分析师对报告发布日后 6至 12个月内行业或公司回报潜力相对基准表现
的预期（A 股市场基准为沪深 300 指数，香港市场基准为恒生指数，美国市场基准为标普 
500 指数，新三板基准指数为三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针对做市转
让标的），北交所基准指数为北证 50指数），具体如下： 
公司投资评级： 
买入：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在 15%以上； 
增持：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于 5%与 15%之间； 
中性：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-15%与-5%之间； 
卖出：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在-15%以下。 
行业投资评级： 
增持： 预期未来 6个月内，行业指数相对强于基准 5%以上； 
中性： 预期未来 6个月内，行业指数相对基准-5%与 5%； 
减持： 预期未来 6个月内，行业指数相对弱于基准 5%以上。 
我们在此提醒您，不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。我们采用的是相
对评级体系，表示投资的相对比重建议。投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定应当充分考虑自身
特定状况，如具体投资目的、财务状况以及特定需求等，并完整理解和使用本报告内容，不
应视本报告为做出投资决策的唯一因素。

2308 台達電(Delta)

報告日期： 2026/11/02 研究員：曾智楷 (Kevin Tseng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 營收 1970.6 億 ， 毛利率34.1%， 營利率16.6%，EPS 9.04
元。受到供應鏈缺料以及拉貨遞延影響， 3Q26 毛利率不如預
期。  
 預估 4Q26 營收將維持成長趨勢，毛利率部分，隨著隨著庫存
預計開始去化，以及部分產品漲價部分產品為反映原物料成本
而調漲價格，毛利率將有望回升。  
 HVDC 部分， ±400V 已在下半年開始出貨， 800V 系統亦持續
發展推進，同時液冷、電源機櫃、 DC-DC 模組等新產品線持續
放量；公司亦持續推進固態氧化物燃料電池 (SOFC) ，目前已有
海外大型客戶接觸送樣，初期產能逾 100MW ，預計 2027 年正
式量產。

一、受缺料影響， 3Q26 毛利率 34.1% ，EPS 9.04 元 
台達電 3Q26 營收 1970.6 億， QoQ +7.5% ，YoY +31.1% ，略低
於公司預期，主要由於供應鏈缺料以及資料中心建置延遲，導致
部分貨品出貨遞延 。 就產品組合來看 ，3Q26 電源及零部件 (Power 
Electronics) 佔整體營收的 54% ，交通 (Mobility) 佔 5% ，自動化
(Automation) 佔 8% 基礎建設 (Infrastructure) 佔 33% 。而獲利方
面， 3Q26 營業毛利 672 億， QoQ +2.8% ，YoY +28.1% ，毛利率
34.1% ，QoQ -1.6ppts ，YoY -0.8ppts ，公司表示，第三季存貨增
加，認列約 39 億元的備抵存貨跌價損失。主要由於現有產品缺
料，以及新產品客戶新平台量產較慢、拉貨延後所致，預計第四
季至明年第一季陸續消化並回沖。 3Q26 營業淨利 326.2 億，營利
率 16.6% ，業外淨利 10.46 億，稅後淨利 234.95 億，單季稅後
EPS 9.04 元。

二、庫存去化、部分產品漲價， 4Q26 營運有望回升  
展望 4Q26 ，公司表示，第四季營收確定將維持成長趨勢，而毛利
率部分，公司亦預估，第四季毛利率將優於第三季，主要由於隨
著庫存預計從第四季開始去化，並在明年第一季陸續消化完畢，
而存貨順利出貨後即可回沖。另外，第四季部分產品為反映原物
料成本而調漲價格 。 預估4Q26 營收 2181 億，QoQ +10.7% ，YoY 
+35.0% ，毛利率 35.5% 。營利率 18.2% ，稅後淨利 283 億，單季
稅後 EPS 10.89 元。

參考價位：1335 元 
     21.3 X 2027 EPS

三. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 37.53/62.49 元 
展望 2026 年，公司表示， 2026 年全年整體資料中心相關營收佔
比將接近 5 成，其中， AI 電源解決方案營收佔比有望達 25% ，液
冷散熱則大約佔 13% 。預估 2026 全年營收 7574 億元， YoY 
+36.5% ，毛利率 35.5% ，營利率 17.3% ，稅後淨利 974.8 億，全
年稅後 EPS 37.53 元。展望 2027 年，受惠於 CSP 持續擴大資本
支出， AI 資料中心建置需求延續，公司預期， AI 相關營收與液冷
系統產品 2027 年的營收佔比將進一步攀升。 HVDC 部分， ±400V
已在下半年開始出貨 ， 由NVIDIA 推動的 800V 系統亦持續發展推
進，同時液冷、電源機櫃、 DC-DC 模組等新產品線持續放量，此
外，公司亦持續推進固態變壓器 (SST) 與固態氧化物燃料電池
(SOFC) ，初期產能逾 100MW ，預計 2027 年正式量產，目前已有
海外大型客戶接觸送樣。而產能部分，公司亦宣布，將於馬來西
亞檳城設立大型生產據點，預計五年內產能追上泰國廠區。另外，
針對 Vicor 提出侵權訴訟以及美國 ITC 啟動調查，公司則表示，
公司自身擁有多項 IP，且 ITC 程序冗長，短期內對公司出貨與客
戶不會造成影響。預估 2027 全年營收 1 兆 695 億元， YoY 
+41.2% ，毛利率 36.5% ，營利率 20.0% ，稅後淨利 1623 億，全
年稅後 EPS 62.49 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 1680 元 
考量到 CSP 持續擴大資本支出， AI 資料中心建置需求延續，公司
具備關鍵產業地位，且漲價效益及遞延訂單後續將陸續反映，將
有望持續挹注公司營收及獲利成長動能。以公司 2027 年 EPS 來
看，目前股價交易於 2027 年 EPS 約 21~22 倍本益比，評價仍具
上修空間，給於買進投資建議，目標價 1680 元(27x 2027 EPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
台達電 (Delta) 成立於 1971 年，以製造繞線式磁性元件起家，早期
專注於筆電 (NB) 、桌機 (DT) 、螢幕 (Monitor) 、伺服器 (Server) 、印
表機 (Printer) 及網通等 IT 相關產品之電源供應器與散熱風扇，憑
藉深厚的核心技術延伸發展，穩居全球磁性與散熱元件重要供應
商及全球最大交換式電源供應器龍頭。因應全球電子產業轉變與
環保節能意識抬頭，台達電近年積極跨足工業自動化、智慧安防
及綠能領域，逐步從關鍵零組件製造商轉型為「整體節能解決方
案提供者」，成為全球電源管理與散熱解決方案的專業領導廠商。
目前集團深耕電源及零組件、自動化與基礎設施等核心業務，並
進一步拓展至交通領域，依 2025 年產品結構比重來看，分別為電

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP 、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google 、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型 (LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28% 更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、 GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中， Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展， 2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。 AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。 Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR 、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

2. 液冷散熱成高階 AI 基礎設施標配，估 2026 年滲透率達 53% 
根據 TrendForce 最新研究顯示，隨著 AI 基礎設施建置浪潮及高
階晶片運算功率不斷攀升，單晶片熱設計功耗已超越 1kW ，使液

AMD 與 Google 等巨頭的高階晶片與整櫃式方案強力推動下，預
估 2026 年液冷散熱於 AI 晶片的滲透率將從 2025 年的 33% 大幅
躍升至 53%，並在 2027 年進一步逼近 60%。其中， Google 因積
極導入自研 TPU 並率先全面推廣整櫃式液冷設計，成為主要推手
之一。此外，隨著 NVIDIA Vera Rubin 平台全面導入全液冷架構，
水冷板、分歧管及冷卻分配系統等關鍵零組件需求隨之大增，帶
動相關供應鏈如 AVC、Cooler Master 等業者積極搶攻各大 CSP
與 AI 平台商機。

圖三、 2023-2027 年液冷散熱於 AI 晶片滲透率預估

資料來源： TrendForce 、宏遠投顧整理

3. 預估 2026 年全球資料中心用電需求年增 31% 
根據  TrendForce 產業研究顯示 ， 受惠於AI 應用需求激增與雲端
服務供應商 (CSP) 加速擴建基礎設施 ， 全球資料中心電力需求容量
預計將由 2025 年的 122.9GW 攀升至 2026 年的 161GW ，YoY 
+31% ，並於 2027 年維持超過 30% 的高成長率；其中， AI 伺服器
為核心增長動能，占整體電力需求比重由 2025 年的 25% 提升至
2026 年的 33.4% ，預計 2027 年更將突破 40% ，同步帶動散熱與
供電等非 IT 設備需求，促使資料中心規劃導入高壓直流供電
(HVDC) 以優化供配電效率。然而，電網供電增速卻未能跟上需求
擴張 ， 全球資料中心電力供需自2026 年起開始背離 ， 並將於2028
年後因電網併網與輸配電建設交付遞延而顯著擴大；預估至 2030
年，全球資料中心電力需求將達 490.7GW ，但電網可供容量僅
222.6GW ，供需缺口高達 268GW( 其中資料中心部署量最大的美
國自 2028 年起遞延加劇，至 2030 年缺口預計超過 170GW) ，未
來此一實質短缺與帳面落差能否透過業者自建發電及電網改善來
填補，仍有待觀察。

### Page 1

ab 30 October 2026
Global Research
Key Call: MediaTek Inc.
Raising TPU guidance with stronger execution
TPU v9 on track to ramp from early 2028
Amid market skepticism on TPU execution, MediaTek delivered better outlook for Q4
and Cloud ASICs, as we previewed. Management raised TPU sales guidance from >US
$2bn to US$2.5bn for 2026, and from US$12-16bn to US$16bn for 2027. This reflects
the steady Google demand for v8t for training, critical to accelerate the new Gemini LLM

development. Importantly, for TPU v9, MediaTek re-affirmed it is on track for high
volume manufacturing in Q128, which clarified  recent noises around delayed tape out.
Our latest understanding is there may be two versions of MediaTek’s TPU v9: the first
version with 336G SerDes should be tape out soon in Nov 2026. There’s a second tape
out which is on track for delivery in Feb 2027. The second version features flexible
SerDes switch between 224G/336G for greater supply flexibility for the whole system.

Conclusion is, we think MediaTek is on good track to scale TPU  sales to our estimates of
US$44bn/53bn in 2028/29  with upside. See Figure 1U B S  f o r e c a s t s  f o r   M e d i a T e k ' s  T P U  b u s i n e s s for our TPU financial analysis.
Thoughts on the second Cloud ASIC client
MediaTek announced the 2nd Cloud ASIC client, which should contribute to revenue in
2028. MediaTek clarified it’s not a Chinese customer. We believe there are 2 higher

possibilities as what we flagged recently. 1) Tesla’s AI6 using Intel 14A, produced at
Terafab ramping in H228. Potential revenue opportunity on full year basis could be ~US
$2bn if MediaTek could ramp 1m units. Margin profile could be lower than TPU's 42%
GM given simpler design. 2) SpaceX/xAI's Dojo3. MediaTek should be partnering with
Nvidia to offer NVLink Fusion for faster time to market. Question is SpaceX/xAI's own
engineering resource and capability for Dojo3 after team reshuffle. If secured, our initial

estimate is revenue opportunity a year could be US$4-9bn, assuming good volume.
Margin profile may be similar or higher than TPU, in our view.
Meaningful operating margin expansion ahead
Q4 sales are guided to grow 28% QoQ at the mid point, beating consensus of 25%,
even with higher base from Q3. GM is guided to edge down to 45.0% at mid point, with
OPM meaningfully rising to 19.0% from 16.1% in Q3. As MediaTek starts ramping TPU
substantially into 2027, the key focus for profitability should shift from GM to OPM due

to different business model vs consumer electronics, and our sense is the market still
under appreciates the structural shift and the stronger earnings power. We forecast
MediaTek's OPM to rise to 25.2%/30.9% in 2027/28 vs consensus of 21.8%/24.2%.
Our 2027/28E EPS estimates are 23%/29% above consensus pre earnings.
Valuation: maintain Key Call Buy and target of NT$7,300
Among our coverage, MediaTek remains the one that we believe the market most

underestimates its execution and opportunity in Cloud AI. We raise 2026E EPS by 5%
and fine tune 2027/28E EPS by 1-2%. Maintain Key Call Buy. Our target of NT$7,300 is
based on 27x 2027-28E PE.

Company Description
MediaTek is a leading integrated circuit (IC) design company in Taiwan, focusing on solutions
from the edge to the cloud, applications for its product offerings range from smartphones,
smart homes and AI PCs to high-performance computing, automotive, and AI data centers.

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target for MediaTek is based on a target PE methodology.
Fabless integrated circuit (IC) design houses face a variety of uncertainties, including rapidly
changing technology, end-market demand, competition, employee turnover and foundry
wafer costs. They are also vulnerable to legal disputes over intellectual property rights. In
addition, MediaTek is exposed to potential price competition in handset SoC, TV SoC, etc.

Downside risks include slower-than-expected market share gains and a weaker-than-
expected demand recovery.

by the analyst to indicate that the stock's price target and/or rating may change in the near term, usually in response to an event that

may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have a 12-month investment horizon.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide an update on AVC. The company plans to release 
its 3Q26 results on 10 November. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call (see our 
latest memo Record-high 3Q26 revenue beat expectations, 7 October). We 
expect AVC to maintain its leading position in the following years with more 
content value from ASICs projects since 4Q26.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results preview. AVC’s 3Q26 revenue of 
TWD59,089m (+20.3% QoQ and +51.8% YoY), a record-high level, 
accounted for 102.3% and 103.0%, respectively, of our previous 
(TWD57,771m, +17.6% QoQ and +48.4% YoY) and the Bloomberg 
consensus forecasts for 3Q26 revenue. We forecast its 3Q26 gross margin 
to be 33.5% (vs. 32.6% in 2Q26 and 26.1% in 3Q25), slightly higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus forecast of 33.3%. We forecast its operating 
profit of TWD16.8bn (+25.0% QoQ and +108.3% YoY), 4.5% higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus forecast of TWD16.1bn and we forecast its 
operating margin of 28.5% (vs. 27.4% in 2Q26 and 20.8% in 3Q25). 
Overall, we forecast its net profit of TWD11.6bn (+21.6% QoQ, +117.8% 
YoY), 4.3% higher than the consensus estimate of TWD11.2bn. For 4Q26, 
we forecast revenue to grow 15.1% QoQ (monthly revenue to rise MoM), 
and 1Q27 revenue to grow 2.9% QoQ considering ASIC and GPU projects.

Solid capacity expansion plan in 2027; worsening supply-demand 
environment to bring in a more favourable price trend to the sector. In 
light of the manifold content value from trays, we now expect content value 
by one of its ASICs clients to increase by another 20%. AVC expects the 
revenue drivers in 2027 to include double-sided cold plates, rising liquid 
cooling penetration from ASICs, and more content value wins (one-stop 
shopping model), for example, from CDU, CPU and NPO. As such, AVC 
plans to ramp up its cold plate capacity by 60% by 3Q27 with more 
automated product lines (positive to its further gross margin YoY 
expansion) to support its Vietnam plants, based on current order visibility 
and high utilisation rate. We keep our 2026-28E EPS unchanged.

What we recommend: We reiterate our Buy (1) call and raise our 12M TP 
to TWD4,815 (from TWD4,465), still based on a target PER of 30x vs. a 
past-3-year PER range of 5-25x [average PER of 15x]) on its solid earnings 
visibility (earnings CAGR of 45.3% over 2026-2028E; 0.66x PEG) and 
applied to our 1-year forward EPS. Downside risks: slower-than-expected 
progress in high-gross-margin orders, slow AI server adoption, and 
competition.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS is 8% above consensus, likely as we are 
relatively more aggressive in our revenue, gross and operating margins.

2 November 2026

3Q26 results preview: likely to beat expectations

➢ We expect AVC’s quarterly revenue to grow QoQ until end of 2027 
➢ More favourable price environment and margins profile in 2027 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call; raising 12M TP to TWD4,815

Company profile 
AVC is a global leading comprehensive thermal solutions provider in segments related to desktops, 
laptops, communication, servers, energy and autos. Its main product line includes thermal products 
(thermal modules and fans), chassis, system assembly and peripheral products and hinges. The 
company has been listed on Taiwan’s main board since September 2002.

### Page 5

5

Asia Vital Components (3017 TT): 2 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 6 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and Chairperson of AVC are the same individual, but we see no issue with its board
quality. Besides, we see the Chairperson has 24 years of experience in this industry, which is a
positive sign from an executive perspective.
Capital management 2 AVC's dividend payout ratio was 40-52% over 2020-25, likely due to its capacity expansion plans.
Related party &
transaction 2 Revenue generated from related-party transactions was 1-3% of total revenue during 2021-25, and we found no suspicious activity.
E Supply chain management 1
AVC has introduced Hazardous Substance Process Management (HSPM) and received
QC080000 certification. In addition, AVC set up a Green Product team to promote HSPM and
formulate environmental regulation to make sure its material sourcing and efficiency are well-
performed.
E Data security 1
To prevent the interruption of information systems and leakage of sensitive information causing
losses to the company and customers, AVC has introduced an information security management
system and passed the ISO 27001 information security management system certification in
2019.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 3

Chroma ATE (2360.TW)
05 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Chroma ATE
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,900 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG. Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical
range as its growth outlook is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its revenue are now AI-
related, with strong growth across product segments including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and well positioned to capitalized on the global AI expansion.

Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth expectation due to changes in capex outlook or
production bottleneck; 2) fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate improves; 3) disappointing
metrology shipment due to changes at ramp-up schedule; 4) testing process change leading to loss in market share; 5) FX risks
(50-60% of Chroma’s sales are in US$, 10-20% in Rmb).
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

雖考量 VR 整體遞延加上中系手機客戶拉貨趨緩，研究部微幅下修 27
年預估，但仍然維持富世達買進投資建議，主係：(1)3Q26 伺服器營收
占比大幅提升至 81%，帶動毛利率達 38.0%，創歷史新高並優於預期；
(2)QD 月產能已擴充至 450 萬顆 （+50%） ， 且2027 年持續擴產 ；(3)2027
年受惠 VR 放量，加上CSP ASIC 專案陸續貢獻，營運成長性高；(4)目
前評價已回落至 17.7 倍，位於低檔。綜上所述，研究部下修 2027 年
營收至 295 億元 （原估316 億元） ，EPS 下修至 121.74 元 （原估124.48
元），給予 24 倍本益比，目標價下調至 2,920 元。

重點摘要： 
1. 產品組合大幅優化，3Q26毛利率躍升至38.0% 
3Q26營收45億元，季增20.4%，年增26.3%，符合預期。其中：
(1)3Q成長主要來自伺服器QD，季增將近8成，主因GB300持續
出貨，加上VR200小量出貨；(2)中系手機部分，受記憶體漲價
及缺貨影響，終端拉貨力道疲弱，單季營收季減51%；(3)滑軌
部分，因通用伺服器記憶體缺料影響，季減將近4成。整體而
言，因QD營收大幅提升，伺服器營收占比由2Q的55%大幅提升
至3Q的81%，產品組合轉佳，故毛利率達38.0%（原估37.0%），
季增4.0ppts，優於預期。營益率32.4%（原估32.4%） ，季增
4.5ppts，符合預期；EPS達18.11元（原估17.57元） ，優於預
期。

2. 4Q26營收微幅下修，惟毛利率續創新高 
展望4Q26，考量中系手機客戶雖為旺季， 但受缺料及漲價影響 ，
拉貨態度偏保守。在VR部分，根據供應鏈調查，若以L11機櫃
組裝端來看，4Q26~1Q27僅小量出貨，真正放量須至2Q27，時
程遞延。但若以零組件端看，液冷QD屬於偏L6端的產品，出貨
時程較早，4Q已開始出貨。但考量放量時程及手機影響，研究
部微幅下修4Q26營收至51億元 （原估53億元） ，季增12.7%，年
增27.6%。毛利率部分，考量伺服器占比可望維持在高檔，上
修毛利率至38.6%（原估37.3%） ，季增0.6ppts；EPS微幅下修
至20.42元（原估20.89元）。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3. NV、ASIC雙引擎並進 
基本面仍維持正向看待，QD成長動能明確：(1)NV VR 4Q逐漸
出貨，動能延續至 27年；下一代Ultra設計將導入更高流量
ZQD(單價翻倍)， 促進價值提升；(2)AWS T3已配合客戶時程出
貨，新世代平台水冷滲透率進一步提升；(3)其他ASIC專案陸
續進入量產，本次新增MSFT Maia QD，預估單櫃約576顆(32顆
*18層)，Google TPU、Meta也陸續出貨。產能方面，QD月產能
已擴充至450萬顆（+50%），2027年持續擴產，預期提升至650
萬顆，足以支撐後續訂單需求。

4. VR放量時程遞延，保守下修2027年營收 
然而 ，研究部仍考量以下因素 ， 保守微幅下修2027年營收預估 ：
(1)NV平台部分，考量VR延後至2Q27放量，恐連帶影響Rubin 
Ultra，且目前機櫃架構亦尚未定案，ZQD用量存在變數；(2)
中系手機客戶整體拉貨動能偏弱， 研究部預估明年衰退，故些
微下修27年預估。

5. 目前評價已回檔，潛在空間浮現，維持買進建議 
綜上所述 ， 研究部下修2026年營收至175億元 （原估178億元） ，
年增41.0%，惟毛利率上修至36.0%（原估35.4%），EPS上修至
64.30元 （原估64.20元）。同步下修2027年營收至295億元 （原
估316億元），EPS微幅下修至121.74元 （原估124.48元） 。目前
評價已回落至17.7倍，位於低檔，潛在空間已浮現，給予24倍
本益比，目標價由3,115元調整至2,920元，維持買進建議。

Document 277: AI股暴跌蔓延亚洲，高净值客户面临追加保证金

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI股暴跌蔓延亚洲，高净值客户面临追加保证金.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-06

### Page 1

⾹港⾼净值客⼾被迫折价接收结构性票据标的股票；⾼盛估算，亚洲选股基⾦上⽉平均亏
损 9.4% 。基⾦经理 Chris McGuire 直⾔：这是互联⽹泡沫以来最极端⾏情，本质是集中仓
位的连锁爆仓，⽽⾮基本⾯重估。
摘要：
美国科技股隔夜急挫，冲击波迅速席卷亚洲市场，对冲基⾦与⾼净值投资者⾸当其冲。
据彭博报道，周五（ 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇），亚洲各地经纪商与基⾦经理被恐慌客⼾的电话淹没， AI 相关仓
位的集中抛售引发连锁反应，部分市场触发熔断机制。据知情⼈⼠透露，持有杠杆头⼨的富裕
客⼾正⾯临追加保证⾦的压⼒。
MSCI 亚太指数周五盘中⼀度下跌 5.3%，此前纳斯达克 100 指数周四单⽇蒸发创纪录的 2.3 万亿美
元市值。韩国综合股价指数（ Kospi ）跌幅⼀度⾼达 8.8% ，触发熔断机制。此轮抛售的背景是，
⺠主党在国会两院取得多数席位后，市场预期其将推动更严格的 AI 监管并放缓数据中⼼建设。
对冲基⾦损失惨重，亚洲股票多头全线受压
此轮 AI 股抛售已在对冲基⾦⾏业造成⼤范围亏损。据彭博报道， ExodusPoint Capital

Management 与 Millennium Management 等⼤型机构均录得损失，尽管部分基⾦从波动中获益。
亚洲市场的整体图景尤为黯淡。据报道， Valliance Asset Management 在致投资者的更新函中
披露，其多空策略主基⾦ 10 ⽉下跌 6.7% 。
⾼盛集团旗下⼤宗经纪业务估算，亚洲股票选股型基⾦上⽉平均亏损 9.4%，将今年以来的累计
涨幅压缩⾄ 14% 。
杠杆头⼨承压，经纪商紧急应对
报道称，⾹港⼀位经纪商表⽰，散⼾连夜涌⼊，向公司提出⼤量咨询，员⼯被迫加班以安抚客
⼾情绪并讨论⻛险管理策略。他坦⾔，对于客⼾⽽⾔，唯⼀的建议就是 “ 尽快出逃 ” ，并要求保证
⾦交易者尽快降低杠杆敞⼝。
不过，该经纪商也指出，由于公司数周前已开始要求客⼾为杠杆科技仓位补充抵押品，⼤规模
强制平仓的⻛险有所降低。
在⽇本，住友三井信托资产管理公司⾸席策略师 Hiroyuki Ueno 表⽰， “ 我们正在应接不暇地处理
有关 AI 抛售的各类询问，卖⽅客⼾正在努⼒向其客⼾解释究竟发⽣了什么。 ” 他同时指出，短期投

资者可以迅速作出反应，但⼤型机构基⾦⾯临的抉择则更为复杂。
对于亚洲众多投资者⽽⾔，当前的核⼼问题已不再是 AI 估值是否需要重置，⽽是此轮抛售究竟
还会延续多久。
T&D Asset Management Co. ⾸席策略师兼基⾦经理 Hiroshi Namioka 表⽰， “ 从我们团队早会的
感受来看，市场正在以⾮理性的⽅式运⾏。我不会说这是彻底的恐慌，但投资者确实很难看到
上⾏空间。 ”

市场参与者还指出，期权相关资⾦流在关键技术位失守后进⼀步放⼤了波动。⾹港独⽴分析

美国市场同步承压， AI 基⾦遭重创
此轮抛售同样重创美国市场的 AI 相关基⾦。华尔街⻅闻⽂章写道， 170 亿美元基⾦ Walleye
Capital 单⽉亏损 5.9% ，被迫裁撤股票多空团队并返还投资者资⾦。系统性对冲基⾦ Engineers
Gate 亦宣布裁撤旗下⼤部分 "Synthesis" 股票团队。根源在于 AI 相关股票仓位⾼度集中，对冲基
⾦群体两周内抛售 200 亿美元科技股。 Millennium 、 Marshall Wace 等多家巨头同步受损。
与此同时，据彭博报道， Rob Pohly 旗下的 Samlyn Capital 已在削减⼏乎所有板块敞⼝（仅保留

全球⾦融股）后，开始裁减⾼级分析师；专注 AI 投资的对冲基⾦ Value Aligned Research
Advisors 上⽉暴跌 33% 。
从业者对此轮⾏情的严峻程度给出了直接评价。 1991 年便开始在芝加哥期权交易所（ CBOT ）
从事期权交易、现任 Eagle's View Capital Management ⽇本可转债套利基⾦经理的 Chris
McGuire 表⽰，“ 这⼏乎是我⾃互联⽹泡沫以来所⻅过的最极端⾏情。持仓集中于相同股票或板
块的股票持有者的集中平仓，制造了我们很⻓时间以来从未⻅过的单向动能。 ”他表⽰，其基⾦
本⽉估计获利 2.5% 或以上。
“ 这看起来更像是仓位爆仓，⽽⾮对未来的根本性重新评估。对部分基⾦⽽⾔，糟糕的 10 ⽉似乎
触发了 11 ⽉初的平仓潮，并形成了连锁效应。 ”

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Exhibit 2: Charting the “Yentervention” and U.S. Treasury Yields Surge Across the Curve.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.
Investment Implications
While some key variables are
complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)
the combination of strong earnings
growth, robust consumer demand
(real final sales to private domestic
purchasers grew at 4.6% for Q2) and
ongoing capital investment suggests
that the fundamental backdrop for
U.S. Equities remains constructive.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.
Exhibit 4: U.S. Exceptionalism: Record Inflows of Portfolio and FDI Inflows.
A) Net Foreign Purchases of US Equities and Investment Funds. B) FDI Inflows to the U.S.

is bullish for U.S. securities, both near-

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

Document 309: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-07)

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

Document 348: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

### Page 4

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

### Page 5

another hike in March, but either way we see renewed cuts in late 2027/early 2028.

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

8. Across the English Channel, we expect the MPC to raise Bank Rate by 25bp to 4% on

### Page 6

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   6
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

Document 351: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Source PDF: all report pdf/How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher
costs and formalising data centres' responsibility to help fund grid
and energy infrastructure upgrades. Such measures could be added
to broader legislation, such as must-pass spending bills needed to
avoid a government shutdown, or a bipartisan package like
permitting reform.
More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase
scrutiny of technology companies, as discussed here, while utilising

the next two years to prepare an AI governance framework for 2028
and beyond.
However, tighter AI and data centre regulation is unlikely to
fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Enabling faster power generation through
permitting reform
Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost
pressures. While Democrats and Republicans diﬀer on the preferred
energy mix, there is still common ground. Technologies likely to
retain bipartisan support include nuclear, geothermal, carbon
capture, and biofuels.
However, the greatest potential for policy action may lie not in
speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.
Today, many energy projects can spend over ﬁve years navigating

the federal permitting process. Around $240bn to $280bn of
infrastructure investment across the eight sectors below enters the

With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

### Page 6

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,
permitting reform is likely to remain a bipartisan theme. The
growing need to expand power supply and infrastructure more
quickly will keep pressure on future Congresses to streamline
permitting.
Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.
Global supply chain capacity distribution
across various sectors

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF, International Energy Agency
This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs
as alternative sources may be more expensive. Solar could
face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

《消費量能未見失速訊號》
• 9月卡片消費YoY回升至+6.2，

1
資產 4Q26 看法 投資展望
核心消費 中立 本行維持產業上下半年營收成長48：52的溫和成長預期，消費產業在旺
季前呈現「量能可望持平、獲利品質分化」，同時零售商備貨行為與終
端消費需求仍支持到貨量的基本盤，但消費者實際支出意願可能受到油
價／利率上升抑制，戰術性減碼受油價與信心承壓較重的旅遊休閒類股
4Q26預計呈「量能可望維持、獲利偏弱」格局
資料來源：美國銀行2026/10/15，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28。

• 消費量能未見失速訊號 P.1
• 「K型」分化持續收斂 P.2
• 4Q26傳統銷售旺季Bottom-Up P.3
• 核心消費三個次產業需求驅動各異 P.4
• 成本與定價動能 P.5
消費產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/10/29
BofA信用卡數據9月卡片消費YoY回升、零售銷售續增(惟
剔除汽油站僅+0.2%),延續8月自7月疲弱中的反彈,為本行
對量販/民生通路保持加碼提供量能基準。
總卡片消費YoY成長率於6-8月連續降溫(6月6.3%→7月
5.0%→8月4.5%),剔除油品後同步走緩
(5.6%→4.3%→3.7%)，主因6月世界盃與線上促銷時序錯
位帶來的高基期效應消退。9月YoY回升至6.2%、剔除油品
為5.1%(BofA指出皆為近四年第三強),惟季調後MoM僅
+0.2%(7月-0.2%、8月+0.9%),剔除油品後幾乎持平,顯示
YoY反彈部分來自油價推升,月度動能趨於溫和。投資意涵：
量能未見明顯失速，不支持對消費產業全面削減曝險,宜改
採結構性次產業輪動

《「K型」分化持續收斂，中低所得消費有收入基礎》
低所得家戶的消費與稅後薪資成長自 7月起皆居三分位
（Tercile）組之首 ，高所得 （不含前5%）已被中 、低所
得超越。三分位組中，低所得家戶的消費與薪資成長皆最
強；前5%仍是消費成長最快的族群 ，但已較高點放緩 。
各組消費成長均高於稅後薪資成長約1個百分點（pp）。

9月各所得階層信用消費成長率 vs. 稅後薪資成長率

《4Q26傳統銷售旺季Bottom-Up需求強弱度分析》
NRF已於10月15日發布2026年假期旺季(11-12月)專屬銷
售預測：零售銷售預估年增4.2%至1.013兆美元(2025年
為+3.1%、9,720億美元；不含汽車經銷商、加油站與餐
飲)，成長率高於去年,但NRF亦提醒,通膨、油價上升與家
庭支出壓力對不同所得族群的影響將有差異。此前3月18
日發布的2026全年預測(+4.4%至5.6兆美元)則早於中東
衝突推高油價與聯準會重啟升息。

重點摘要
零售客流量表
5
民生必需品：P&G有機成長 +4.0%中，銷量貢獻 +2.4%，
價格貢獻有限；引用的 NielsenIQ資料也顯示家庭與個人
護理類 +4.3%、包裝食品 −0.9%。成長主要來自銷量與
組合而非漲價，且品類間分化明顯，這是本行僅給予中性
評等的主因。
餐飲：2Q26同店銷售呈現明顯分化 ，本行預估客流領先
的連鎖與咖啡系統維持正成長 ，倚賴折價促銷的速食與披
薩鏈許多為負成長 。在外食價格比居家食品高出約 1.7個
百分點的環境下 ，客流與價格的取捨將是 4Q26餐飲評等
的主要變數。
《成本與定價動能》
9月CPI顯示，整體CPI月增 +0.6%、年增 +3.7%，核心
CPI年增 +2.6%；能源年增 +20.4%、汽油年增 +34.2%，

是整體通膨上行的主因。對核心消費而言，關鍵在於食品
與能源的分離：居家食品月增 −0.1%、年增 +1.8%，外
食年增 +3.5%。
對毛利的含意：能源成本上升而零售端食品價格溫和 ，代
表核心消費業者較難以轉嫁成本 ，壓力更可能先反映在毛
利率而非銷售額 。P&G在指引中揭露本財年約 14億美元
稅後成本逆風（含其他逆風合計約每股0.79美元，約拖累
核心EPS成長11%），為公司自行揭露數字 ，本行未重新
計算 。Kimberly-Clark則預期 2026年有機銷售成長約落
後 所 屬 品 類 加 權 成 長100bp ， 顯示在品類成長僅約
+1.7% 的環境下價格與組合的操作空間有限 。成長動能
偏向「量」而非「價」；此結構有利於營收穩定，但對毛
利率的支撐較弱。

Document 496: イランの緊張長期化と日米欧の金融政策 ～インフレ再燃で問われる金融引き締めの行方～
